CR 24 (XP 62,000; PB +7)

Traits

Amphibious. The old pressure can breathe air and water.

Legendary Resistance (3/Day). If the old pressure fails a saving throw, it can choose to succeed instead.

Actions

Multiattack. The old pressure makes two Bite attacks. It can replace one attack with a use of Pressure Snap.

Bite. Melee Attack Roll: +16, reach 15 ft. 22 (3d8 + 9) Piercing damage plus 14 (4d6) Thunder damage.

Drowning Song (Recharge 5-6). Constitution Saving Throw: DC 24, each creature in a 60-foot Cone. Failure: 78 (12d12) Thunder damage, and the target has the Deafened condition until the end of its next turn. Success: Half damage only.

Pressure Snap. Strength Saving Throw: DC 24, one creature within 30 feet that is Deafened. Failure: 63 (14d8) Thunder damage. Success: Half damage.

Legendary Actions

Legendary Action Uses: 3. Immediately after another creature's turn, the old pressure can expend a use to take one of the following actions. It regains all expended uses at the start of each of its turns.

Breach Strike. The old pressure moves up to half its Speed without provoking Opportunity Attacks, and it makes one Bite attack.

Rolling Slam. Strength Saving Throw: DC 24, each creature within 10 feet of the old pressure. Failure: The target has the Prone condition.

[concept] identity: The leviathan opens from below, rising under a hull to take it in its jaws while its subsonic song rolls outward and stalls the fleet around it. Every round after it circles and slams with a body like wet stone, layering that same drowning song over the ships still afloat until oars and voices die. It is a patient, territorial killer — it does not chase, it herds a fleet into the deep and fights to the death, since nothing at this depth can hurt it and live.
[concept] lore: The eldest thing in the trench remembers when this sea was a valley, and it sings the old pressure of that drowned dark down the currents to drag anything that dares float above its roof into the depths with it.

+2

Mire-Slicked Hide. Difficult terrain composed of bog mud doesn't cost the mire hound extra movement, and the mire hound can move across bog mud without needing to make an ability check.
Whelm. As a bonus action, the mire hound whelms one Medium or smaller creature it is grappling. While whelmed, the target is blinded and restrained, has total cover against attacks and other effects outside the mire hound, and takes 3 (1d6) bludgeoning damage at the start of each of the mire hound's turns. The mire hound can have only one creature whelmed at a time. If the mire hound takes 5 damage or more in a single turn from the whelmed creature, the mire hound must release the target at the end of that turn, and the target exits prone in an unoccupied space within 5 feet of the mire hound. If the mire hound dies, a whelmed creature pulls free prone by using 5 feet of movement.

Actions
Multiattack: The mire hound makes two Bite attacks. It can replace one attack with a use of Bog Maw Lash.
Bite: Melee Weapon Attack: +3 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. Hit: 5 (1d8 + 1) piercing damage. If the target is a Large or smaller creature, it is grappled (escape DC 11). While grappled, the target is restrained.
Bog Maw Lash: One creature within 30 feet that is grappled by the mire hound must make a DC 13 Strength saving throw, taking 11 (2d10) piercing damage on a failed save, or half as much damage on a successful one.
```

Immunities Necrotic, Poison; Charmed, Exhaustion, Frightened, Grappled, Paralyzed, Petrified, Poisoned, Prone, Restrained

Senses darkvision 60 ft.; Passive Perception 13

Languages Common plus one other language

CR 3 (XP 700; PB +2)

Traits

Bell-Rope Reprisal. A creature that hits the ringer with a melee attack while within 5 feet of it takes 5 (1d10) Thunder damage and has the Deafened condition until the end of its next turn.

Ethereal Sight. The ringer can see 60 feet into the Ethereal Plane when it is on the Material Plane.

Incorporeal Movement. The ringer can move through other creatures and objects as if they were Difficult Terrain. It takes 5 (1d10) Force damage if it ends its turn inside an object.

Actions

Multiattack. The ringer makes two Withering Touch attacks. It can replace one attack with a use of Horrific Visage.

Withering Touch. Melee Attack Roll: +5, reach 5 ft. 19 (3d10 + 3) Necrotic damage.

Knell Tolling (Recharge 5-6). Constitution Saving Throw: DC 13, each creature in a 15-foot Emanation originating from the ringer. Failure: 13 (3d8) Thunder damage, and the target has the Deafened condition until the end of its next turn. Success: Half damage only.

Horrific Visage. Wisdom Saving Throw: DC 13, each creature in a 60-foot Cone that can see the ringer and isn't an Undead. Failure: 10 (2d6 + 3) Psychic damage, and the target has the Frightened condition until the start of the ringer's next turn. Success: The target is immune to this ghost's Horrific Visage for 24 hours.

Possession (Recharge 6). Charisma Saving Throw: DC 13, one Humanoid the ringer can see within 5 feet. Failure: The target is possessed by the ringer; the ringer disappears, and the target has the Incapacitated condition and loses control of its body. The ringer now controls the body, but the target retains awareness. The ringer can't be targeted by any attack, spell, or other effect, except ones that specifically target Undead. The ringer's game statistics are the same, except it uses the possessed target's Speed, as well as the target's Strength, Dexterity, and Constitution modifiers.
The possession lasts until the body drops to 0 Hit Points or the ringer leaves as a Bonus Action. When the possession ends, the ringer appears in an unoccupied space within 5 feet of the target, and the target is immune to this ghost's Possession for 24 hours. Success: The target is immune to this ghost's Possession for 24 hours.

Etherealness. The ringer casts the Etherealness spell, requiring no spell components and using Charisma as the spellcasting ability. The ringer is visible on the Material Plane while on the Border Ethereal and vice versa, but it can't affect or be affected by anything on the other plane.

[concept] identity: The climbing owlbear begins fights from above, dropping off cliff faces and treetrunk with a shrieking ambush that scatters its prey before it closes, and it never stays in one place, springing up a wall or over a ledge to strike again from a new angle. It fights in loose tandem with others of its kind, feinting and harrying so one of them can land the crushing grapple, and when bloodied it does not retreat so much as vanish upward, clinging to stone and waiting to fall on whatever follows. This is a cave-and-cliff monstrosity whose hooked forelimbs and beaked skull were made to rend and hold, and it fights the way that body was built — a raking hit-and-run predator that turns any vertical surface into its hunting ground.
[concept] lore: These owlbears den in the high cracks of canyon and spire, and they climb not out of fear but appetite — the pack that owns a cliff face owns everything below it. When it was named, the name was stolen from the older creature that already bore it.

```
Pit Slide
Large elemental, neutral
Armor Class

13 (natural armor)

Hit Points: 

93 (11d10 + 33)

Speed: 

30 ft., burrow 30 ft.

STR	DEX	CON	INT	WIS	CHA


16 (+3)

	

8 (-1)

	

16 (+3)

	

4 (-3)

	

10 (+0)

	

4 (-3)

Skills: 

Athletics +6, Perception +3

Saving Throws: 

STR +6

Damage Immunities: 

poison

Condition Immunities: 

exhaustion, poisoned

Senses: 

tremorsense 60 ft., passive Perception 13

Languages: 

None

Challenge

5 (1,800 XP)

Proficiency Bonus:

+3

Sure Footing. Difficult terrain composed of scree and loose rubble doesn't cost the pit slide extra movement, and the pit slide can move across scree and loose rubble without needing to make an ability check.
Entomb. As a bonus action, the pit slide entombs one Medium or smaller creature it is grappling. While entombed, the target is blinded and restrained, has total cover against attacks and other effects outside the pit slide, and takes 7 (2d6) thunder damage at the start of each of the pit slide's turns. The pit slide can have only one creature entombed at a time. If the pit slide takes 15 damage or more in a single turn from the entombed creature, the pit slide must release the target at the end of that turn, and the target exits prone in an unoccupied space within 5 feet of the pit slide. If the pit slide dies, an entombed creature breaks free prone by using 5 feet of movement.

Actions
Multiattack: The pit slide makes two attacks: one with its Scree and one with its Quarry Bury.
Scree: Melee Weapon Attack: +6 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. Hit: 10 (2d6 + 3) bludgeoning damage plus 7 (2d6) thunder damage. If the target is a Large or smaller creature, it is grappled (escape DC 14). While grappled, the target is restrained.
Quarry Entombment (Recharge 5-6): One Medium or smaller creature within 5 feet of the pit slide that it hit with a melee attack this turn must make a DC 14 Dexterity saving throw. On a failed save, the target takes 17 (5d6) thunder damage and is swallowed. A swallowed creature is blinded and restrained, has total cover against attacks and other effects outside the pit slide, and takes 9 (2d8) thunder damage at the start of each of the pit slide's turns. If the pit slide takes 15 damage or more on a single turn from a creature inside it, it must succeed on a DC 14 Constitution saving throw at the end of that turn or regurgitate all swallowed creatures, each of which falls prone in a space within 5 feet of the pit slide. If the pit slide dies, a swallowed creature is no longer restrained and can escape from the corpse using 10 feet of movement, exiting prone.
Quarry Bury: Melee Weapon Attack: +6 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. Hit: 10 (2d6 + 3) bludgeoning damage, and the target is restrained until the end of its next turn.
```

Actions

Spellcasting: The red dragon casts one of the following spells, requiring no material components and using Charisma as the spellcasting ability (spell save DC 20, +12 to hit with spell attacks): -

At will: command, detect magic, scorching ray

1/day each: fireball
Fire Breath (Recharge 5-6): Each creature in a 60-foot cone must make a DC 21 Dexterity saving throw, taking 59 (17d6) fire damage on a failed save, or half as much damage on a successful one.
Legendary Actions

The red dragon can take 3 legendary actions, choosing from the options below. Only one legendary action option can be used at a time and only at the end of another creature's turn. The red dragon regains spent legendary actions at the start of its turn.

Commanding Presence: The red dragon uses Spellcasting to cast command (cast at 2nd level). The red dragon can't take this action again until the start of its next turn.
Fiery Rays: The red dragon uses Spellcasting to cast scorching ray. The red dragon can't take this action again until the start of its next turn.
Pounce: The red dragon moves up to half its speed, and it makes one Rend attack.

Traits

Bright Seams. A creature that touches the brass tyrant or hits it with a melee attack while within 5 feet of it takes 10 (3d6) Fire damage.

Legendary Resistance (3/Day). If the brass tyrant fails a saving throw, it can choose to succeed instead.

Immutable Form. The brass tyrant can't shape-shift.

Siege Monster. The brass tyrant deals double damage to objects and structures.

Actions

Multiattack. The brass tyrant makes two Brass Fist attacks. It can replace one attack with a use of Merciless Strike.

Brass Fist. Melee Attack Roll: +12, reach 10 ft. 20 (4d6 + 6) Bludgeoning damage plus 14 (4d6) Fire damage.

Forge-Heat (Recharge 5-6). Wisdom Saving Throw: DC 19, each creature in a 30-foot Cone. Failure: 72 (16d8) Fire damage, and the target has the Frightened condition until the end of its next turn. Success: Half damage only.

Merciless Strike. Wisdom Saving Throw: DC 19, one creature within 60 feet that has the Frightened condition. Failure: 71 (13d10) Fire damage, and the target has the Frightened condition until the end of its next turn. Success: Half damage.

Legendary Actions

Legendary Action Uses: 3. Immediately after another creature's turn, the brass tyrant can expend a use to take one of the following actions. It regains all expended uses at the start of each of its turns.

Stride and Crush. The brass tyrant moves up to half its Speed without provoking Opportunity Attacks, and it makes one Brass Fist attack.

Trample Ruin. The brass tyrant moves up to half its Speed without provoking Opportunity Attacks. During this movement, it can move through Large or smaller objects and structures, destroying them.

Quake Step. Strength Saving Throw: DC 19, each creature within 10 feet of the brass tyrant. Failure: The target has the Prone condition.

[concept] identity: A cathedral's worth of funeral bells, torn loose and welded into a single iron flock, descends on a battlefield and marks the dying by sound — each bell tolls only when it finds a body that is close enough to death to be claimed. Once the swarm has chosen, it settles over the fallen in a grinding, chiming canopy, ringing louder as the victim's heart slows, and it does not leave until the corpse is cold and the last note has been rung. Wounded and cloven, it splits into two lesser flocks that keep ringing, mending their broken bells from the iron of the fallen's armor and weapons — patient, joyless, and utterly unwilling to abandon a marked body.
[concept] lore: Forged by a plague-town's bellfounder who poured every funeral toll of a dying city into one iron peal, the swarm now seeks out the last, worst note ever struck — it wants to hear the world's final bell toll.

[concept] identity: The hydra wades into reach and bites with every head it still has, and each head it loses leaves a smoking stump pouring stinging vapour into the air around it. Every round it either feasts on a nearby wound to close its own or sweeps its remaining heads in a ragged fan, and as stumps multiply the creature becomes a walking hazard as much as a brawler. It does not regenerate — it cannibalises its own smoke-scarred flesh to seal what remains, fighting louder and hotter the more it is hurt until it dies mid-lunge.
[concept] lore: Bred in a vent-choked marsh where nothing regrows clean, the hydra learned to burn its own wounds shut, and it hunts now not to eat but to keep adding smoke to the shroud it hides in.

Thunder Absorption. Whenever the timber ram is subjected to Thunder damage, it takes no damage and instead regains a number of Hit Points equal to the Thunder damage dealt.

Immutable Form. The timber ram can't shape-shift.

Magic Resistance. The timber ram has Advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.

Actions

Multiattack. The timber ram makes two Timber Slam attacks.

Timber Slam. Melee Attack Roll: +8, reach 5 ft. 27 (5d8 + 5) Bludgeoning damage.

Bonus Actions

Thunder Retort. One creature within 5 feet that hit the timber ram with an attack roll since the end of the timber ram's last turn takes 11 (2d10) Thunder damage.

Vampire
Medium undead, lawful evil
Armor Class

16 (natural armor)

Hit Points: 

195 (23d8 + 92)

Speed: 

40 ft., climb 40 ft.

STR	DEX	CON	INT	WIS	CHA


18 (+4)

	

18 (+4)

	

18 (+4)

	

17 (+3)

	

15 (+2)

	

18 (+4)

Skills: 

Perception +7, Stealth +9

Saving Throws: 

DEX +9, INT +8

Damage Resistances: 

necrotic

Damage Immunities: 

poison

Condition Immunities: 

exhaustion, poisoned

Senses: 

darkvision 120 ft., passive Perception 17

Languages: 

Common plus two other languages

Challenge

13 (10,000 XP)

Proficiency Bonus:

+5

Legendary Resistance (3/Day, or 4/Day in Lair). If the vampire fails a saving throw, it can choose to succeed instead.
Misty Escape. If the vampire drops to 0 hit points outside its resting place, the vampire uses Shape-Shift to become mist (no action required). If it can't use Shape-Shift, it is destroyed.
While it has 0 hit points in mist form, it can't return to its vampire form, and it must reach its resting place within 2 hours or be destroyed. Once in its resting place, it returns to its vampire form and is paralyzed until it regains any hit points, and it regains 1 hit point after spending 1 hour there.
Spider Climb. The vampire can climb difficult surfaces, including along ceilings, without needing to make an ability check.
Vampire Weakness. The vampire has these weaknesses: Forbiddance: the vampire can't enter a residence without an invitation from an occupant.
Running Water: the vampire takes 20 acid damage if it ends its turn in running water.
Stake to the Heart: If a weapon that deals piercing damage is driven into the vampire's heart while the vampire is incapacitated in its resting place, the vampire is paralyzed until the weapon is removed.
Sunlight: the vampire takes 20 radiant damage if it starts its turn in sunlight. While in sunlight, it has disadvantage on attack rolls and ability checks.
Shape-Shift. As a bonus action, if the vampire isn't in sunlight or running water, it shape-shifts into a Tiny bat (Speed 5 ft., Fly Speed 30 ft.) or a Medium cloud of mist (Speed 5 ft., Fly Speed 20 ft. [hover]), or it returns to its vampire form. Anything it is wearing transforms with it.
While in bat form, the vampire can't speak. Its game statistics, other than its size and Speed, are unchanged.
While in mist form, the vampire can't take any actions, speak, or manipulate objects. It is weightless and can enter an enemy's space and stop there. If air can pass through a space, the mist can do so, but it can't pass through liquid. It has resistance to all damage, except the damage it takes from sunlight.
Charm (Recharge 5-6). As a bonus action, the vampire casts charm person, requiring no spell components and using Charisma as the spellcasting ability (spell save DC 17), and the duration is 24 hours. The charmed target is a willing recipient of the vampire's Bite, the damage of which doesn't end the spell. When the spell ends, the target is unaware it was charmed by the vampire.

Actions
Multiattack: The vampire makes two Grave Strike attacks and uses Bite.
Grave Strike: Melee Weapon Attack: +9 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. Hit: 8 (1d8 + 4) bludgeoning damage plus 7 (2d6) necrotic damage. If the target is a Large or smaller creature, it is grappled (escape DC 17) by one of two hands.
Bite (Bat or Vampire Form Only): one creature within 5 feet that is willing or that is grappled, incapacitated, or restrained must make a DC 17 Constitution saving throw, taking 6 (1d4 + 4) piercing damage plus 13 (3d8) necrotic damage on a failed save. The target's hit point maximum decreases by an amount equal to the necrotic damage taken, and the vampire regains hit points equal to that amount. A humanoid reduced to 0 hit points by this damage and then buried rises the following sunset as a vampire spawn under the vampire's control.
```

Traits

Flyby. The wound archon doesn't provoke Opportunity Attacks when it flies out of an enemy's reach.

Opportunist's Flight. When a creature the wound archon can see ends its turn within 5 feet of it, the wound archon can move up to 10 feet without provoking Opportunity Attacks.

Actions

Multiattack. The wound archon makes three Sting attacks.

Sting. Melee Attack Roll: +10, reach 10 ft. 14 (2d8 + 5) Piercing damage plus 10 (3d6) Poison damage. If the wound archon flew at least 20 feet in a straight line toward the target immediately before the hit, the target takes an extra 7 (2d6) Piercing damage and, if it is Large or smaller, has the Prone condition.

Swarm Column (Recharge 5-6). The wound archon flies up to its Speed without provoking Opportunity Attacks. Each creature it passes within 5 feet during this movement is targeted once by the following effect. Strength Saving Throw: DC 17. Failure: 67 (15d8) Poison damage, and the target has the Prone condition. Success: Half damage.

Bonus Actions

Aerial Agility. The wound archon takes the Dash or Disengage action.

Droning War-Hymn (2/Day). Each ally within 30 feet of the wound archon gains 13 (3d8) Temporary Hit Points.

CR 15 (XP 13,000; PB +5)

Traits

Tunneler. The purple worm can burrow through solid rock at half its Burrow Speed and leaves a 10-foot-diameter tunnel in its wake.

Actions

Multiattack. The purple worm makes one Bite attack and one Tail Stinger attack. It can replace one attack with a use of Caustic Gulp.

Bite. Melee Attack Roll: +14, reach 10 ft. 22 (3d8 + 9) Piercing damage. If the target is a Large or smaller creature, it has the Grappled condition (escape DC 22), and it has the Restrained condition until the grapple ends.

Tail Stinger. Melee Attack Roll: +14, reach 10 ft. 16 (2d6 + 9) Piercing damage plus 35 (10d6) Poison damage.

Caustic Gulp. Dexterity Saving Throw: DC 21, one Large or smaller creature within 10 feet that the purple worm hit with a melee attack this turn. Failure: 42 (12d6) Acid damage, and the target is swallowed. A swallowed creature has the Blinded and Restrained conditions, has Total Cover against attacks and other effects outside the purple worm, and takes 22 (5d8) Acid damage at the start of each of the purple worm's turns. If the purple worm takes 40 damage or more on a single turn from a creature inside it, it must succeed on a DC 21 Constitution saving throw at the end of that turn or regurgitate all swallowed creatures, each of which falls in a space within 10 feet of the purple worm and has the Prone condition. If the purple worm dies, a swallowed creature is no longer Restrained and can escape from the corpse using 15 feet of movement, exiting with the Prone condition.

Bonus Actions

Swallow. Strength Saving Throw: DC 21, one Large or smaller creature Grappled by the purple worm (it can have up to three creatures swallowed at a time). Failure: The target is swallowed by the purple worm, and the Grappled condition ends. A swallowed creature has the Blinded and Restrained conditions, has Total Cover against attacks and other effects outside the purple worm, and takes 17 (5d6) Acid damage at the start of each of the purple worm's turns.
If the purple worm takes 30 damage or more on a single turn from a creature inside it, the purple worm must succeed on a DC 21 Constitution saving throw at the end of that turn or regurgitate all swallowed creatures, each of which falls in a space within 5 feet of the purple worm and has the Prone condition. If the purple worm dies, any swallowed creature no longer has the Restrained condition and can escape from the corpse using 20 feet of movement, exiting Prone.

[concept] identity: The Pit Fiend does not swing for the kill — it swings to bring a knight to the threshold of death, then crouches over the fallen and wrings the last oath from his lips as a binding on the chain it drags behind it. Every round it presses the same slow, patient assault, herding foes into the dying state its law requires, and it treats strikes against it as entries being added to a ledger it will collect on later. Wounded, it does not flee; it grows wrathful and greedy, fighting to the death because a foe who dies unsworn is a debt it intends to make good.
[concept] lore: Forged in the ash-pits below the muster-fields of Hell, the Pit Fiend is Hell's oath-collector, sent to harvest the final promises of dying knights so that every broken vow swells its chain into a bond it can call upon.

Soverign of the Bone Sun
Medium undead, neutral evil
Armor Class

20 (natural armor)

Hit Points: 

315 (42d8 + 126)

Speed: 

30 ft.

STR	DEX	CON	INT	WIS	CHA


11 (+0)

	

16 (+3)

	

16 (+3)

	

21 (+5)

	

14 (+2)

	

16 (+3)

Skills: 

Arcana +17, History +11, Insight +8, Perception +8

Saving Throws: 

CON +9, INT +11, WIS +8

Damage Resistances: 

cold, lightning

Damage Immunities: 

necrotic, poison

Condition Immunities: 

charmed, exhaustion, frightened, paralyzed, poisoned

Senses: 

darkvision 120 ft., passive Perception 18

Languages: 

All

Challenge

20 (25,000 XP)

Proficiency Bonus:

+6

Legendary Resistance (3/Day). If the soverign fails a saving throw, it can choose to succeed instead.
Spirit Jar. If destroyed, the soverign reforms in 1d10 days if it has a spirit jar, reviving with all its hit points. The new body appears in an unoccupied space within the soverign's lair.
Protective Magic (3/Day) (Reaction). When the soverign is hit by an attack roll, the soverign casts shield in response, gaining a +5 bonus to AC until the start of its next turn, including against the triggering attack.

Actions
Multiattack: The soverign makes three attacks, using Eldritch Burst or Paralyzing Touch.
Eldritch Burst: Melee or Ranged Spell Attack: +11 to hit, reach 5 ft. or range 120 ft., one target. Hit: 31 (4d12 + 5) force damage.
Paralyzing Touch: Melee Spell Attack: +11 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. Hit: 15 (3d6 + 5) cold damage, and the target is paralyzed until the start of the soverign's next turn.
Spellcasting: The soverign casts one of the following spells, using Intelligence as the spellcasting ability (spell save DC 19): -

At will: detect magic, detect thoughts, dispel magic, fireball, invisibility, lightning bolt, mage hand, prestidigitation

2/day each: animate dead, dimension door, plane shift

1/day each: chain lightning, finger of death, power word kill, scrying
Legendary Actions

Soverign can take 3 legendary actions, choosing from the options below. Only one legendary action option can be used at a time and only at the end of another creature's turn. Soverign regains spent legendary actions at the start of its turn.

Deathly Teleport: The soverign teleports up to 60 feet to an unoccupied space it can see, and each creature within 10 feet of the space it left takes 11 (2d10) necrotic damage.
Disrupt Life: Each creature that isn't an undead within 20 feet of the soverign must make a DC 19 Constitution saving throw, taking 31 (9d6) necrotic damage on a failed save, or half as much damage on a successful one. Whether it fails or succeeds, the soverign can't take this action again until the start of its next turn.
Frightening Gaze: The soverign casts fear, using the same spellcasting ability as Spellcasting. The soverign can't take this action again until the start of its next turn.
```

Amphibious. The gold dragon can breathe air and water.

Actions
Multiattack: The gold dragon makes three Rend attacks. It can replace one attack with a use of (A) Spellcasting to cast Guiding Bolt (level 4 version) or (B) Weakening Breath.
Rend: Melee Weapon Attack: +17 to hit, reach 15 ft., one target. Hit: 19 (2d8 + 10) slashing damage plus 9 (2d8) fire damage.
Spellcasting: The gold dragon casts one of the following spells, requiring no material components and using Charisma as the spellcasting ability (spell save DC 24, +16 to hit with spell attacks): -

At will: detect magic, guiding bolt, shapechange

1/day each: flame strike, word of recall, zone of truth
Fire Breath (Recharge 5-6): Each creature in a 90-foot cone must make a DC 24 Dexterity saving throw, taking 71 (13d10) fire damage on a failed save, or half as much damage on a successful one.
Weakening Breath: Each creature that isn't currently affected by this breath in a 90-foot cone must make a DC 24 Strength saving throw. On a failed save, the target has disadvantage on Strength-based d20 tests and subtracts 5 (1d10) from its damage rolls. It repeats the save at the end of each of its turns, ending the effect on itself on a success. After 1 minute, it succeeds automatically.
Legendary Actions

The gold dragon can take 3 legendary actions, choosing from the options below. Only one legendary action option can be used at a time and only at the end of another creature's turn. The gold dragon regains spent legendary actions at the start of its turn.

Banish: One creature the gold dragon can see within 120 feet must make a DC 24 Charisma saving throw. On a failed save, the target takes 24 (7d6) force damage, and the target is incapacitated and is transported to a harmless demiplane until the start of the gold dragon's next turn, at which point it reappears in an unoccupied space of the gold dragon's choice within 120 feet of the gold dragon. Whether it succeeds or fails, the gold dragon can't take this action again until the start of its next turn.
Guiding Light: The gold dragon uses Spellcasting to cast guiding bolt (cast at 4th level).
Pounce: The gold dragon moves up to half its speed, and it makes one Rend attack.

CR 9 (XP 5,000; PB +4)

Traits

Caustic Carapace. A creature that touches the hivecrawler or hits it with a melee attack while within 5 feet of it takes 7 (2d6) Acid damage.

Actions

Multiattack. The hivecrawler makes two attacks, using Scythe or Carving Limbs in any combination.

Scythe. Melee Attack Roll: +8, reach 10 ft. 31 (6d8 + 4) Slashing damage. If the target is a Large or smaller creature, it has the Grappled condition (escape DC 16). While Grappled, the target has the Restrained condition.

Seething Spray (Recharge 5-6). Constitution Saving Throw: DC 15, each creature in a 30-foot Cone. Failure: 32 (5d12) Acid damage, and the target has the Poisoned condition until the end of its next turn. Success: Half damage only.

Carving Limbs. Melee Attack Roll: +8, reach 5 ft. 31 (6d8 + 4) Slashing damage.

Bonus Actions

Swallow. The hivecrawler swallows one Medium or smaller creature it is grappling. While swallowed, the target has the Blinded and Restrained conditions, has Total Cover against attacks and other effects outside the hivecrawler, and takes 10 (3d6) Acid damage at the start of each of the hivecrawler's turns. The hivecrawler can have only one creature swallowed at a time. If the hivecrawler takes 25 damage or more on a single turn from the swallowed creature, the hivecrawler must regurgitate the target at the end of that turn, and the target exits Prone in an unoccupied space within 10 feet of the hivecrawler. If the hivecrawler dies, a swallowed creature exits the body Prone by using 10 feet of movement.

Threshold Warden
Huge construct, lawful neutral
Armor Class

20 (natural armor)

Hit Points: 

168 (16d12 + 64)

Speed: 

20 ft.

STR	DEX	CON	INT	WIS	CHA


18 (+4)

Common

11 (7,200 XP)

+4

Iron Rebound. A creature that hits the warden with a melee attack while within 5 feet of it takes 10 (3d6) thunder damage and is deafened until the end of its next turn.
Ward Bond. The warden is magically bound to a ward of its choosing, and it can have only one ward at a time. While the warden is within 60 feet of its ward, half of any damage the ward takes (round up) is transferred to the warden.
Regeneration. The warden regains 10 hit points at the start of each of its turns if it has at least 1 hit point.
Answering Sting. As a bonus action, the warden deals 10 (3d6) force damage to one creature within 5 feet that hit the warden with an attack since the end of the warden's last turn.
Interpose (Reaction). When a creature within 5 feet of the warden that it guards is hit by an attack, the attack hits the warden instead, and the warden has resistance to the attack's damage.

Actions
Multiattack: The warden makes two Iron Fist attacks.
Iron Fist: Melee Weapon Attack: +8 to hit, reach 10 ft., one target. Hit: 25 (6d6 + 4) bludgeoning damage.
Ward's Bastion (2/Day): The warden targets itself or one creature it can see within 60 feet. The target gains 24 (7d6) temporary hit points, and it gains a +2 bonus to AC until the end of the warden's next turn.

Actions

Multiattack. The iron templar makes three Mace attacks. It can replace one attack with a use of Shield Bash.

Mace. Melee Attack Roll: +7, reach 5 ft. 17 (3d8 + 4) Bludgeoning damage, and the target has Disadvantage on its next attack roll before the start of the iron templar's next turn.

Shield Bash. Strength Saving Throw: DC 15, one creature within 5 feet that the iron templar can see. Failure: 9 (2d4 + 4) Bludgeoning damage. If the target is a Medium or smaller creature, it has the Prone condition.

[concept] identity: This templar priest wades in behind a raised shield, mace held high, and opens by planting himself between the party's squishies and whatever wants them dead. Every round he spends is a measured toll: a shield shove to pin the enemy in reach, then a percussive mace blow to the knee, the elbow, the jaw — blows meant to disable and displace, never to kill cleanly. Bloodied, he does not flee; he backs into a corner and makes the doorway cost double, and his grim satisfaction is a heretic sent crawling out instead of a corpse dropped.
[concept] lore: The Iron Orders forge their templars by oath and by repetitive strike rather than by miracle — each is a piece of iron discipline given a mace and told that faith is what the body does when it refuses to step aside.

Medium humanoid, neutral

17 (splint)

170 (31d8 + 31)

30 ft.

+4

Magic Resistance. Ilvareth has advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.
Misty Step (3/Day). As a bonus action, Ilvareth casts misty step, using the same spellcasting ability as Spellcasting.
Protective Magic (3/Day) (Reaction). When Ilvareth is hit by an attack, Ilvareth casts shield in response, gaining a +5 bonus to AC until the start of its next turn, including against the triggering attack.

Actions
Multiattack: Ilvareth makes four Arcane Burst attacks.
Spellcasting: Ilvareth casts one of the following spells, using Intelligence as the spellcasting ability (spell save DC 17): -

At will: detect magic, detect thoughts, disguise self, invisibility, light, mage armor, mage hand, prestidigitation

2/day each: fly, lightning bolt

1/day each: cone of cold, mind blank, scrying, teleport
Arcane Burst: Melee or Ranged Spell Attack: +9 to hit, reach 5 ft. or range 150 ft., one target. Hit: 27 (4d10 + 5) force damage.

Every round after, he refuses to be cornered: he blinks through shadow to re-range, trading single-target bolts for cones of cold, hungry murk, and keeps a mirrored double between himself and the nearest blade. Under pressure he grows cruelly playful, taunting a bloodied foe from three shadows at once while he steps ever closer to the treeline and the dark road out.

Actions

Multiattack. The draught makes two Claw attacks. It can replace one attack with a use of Draught of Vitality.

Draught of Vitality. Constitution Saving Throw: DC 14, one creature within 30 feet. Failure: 18 (4d8) Necrotic damage, and the draught regains Hit Points equal to half the damage dealt. Success: Half damage only.

Bonus Actions

False Form. The draught shape-shifts to resemble an ordinary object or fixture of the place it waits in while retaining its game statistics, or it returns to its true form. While it is motionless in its false form, it is indistinguishable from that feature.

[concept] identity: The thing in the well waits at the bottom of its shaft, a sodden corpse-shape mistaken for a log or an old bucket rope until a hand reaches down into the dark — then it lurches up the wet stones to seize the wrist and drag the victim over the lip. Each round it hauls down and drowns what it has caught, clamping on and sinking with its prey, and if wounded it clings tighter and descends toward the bottom of the shaft rather than releasing. It does not toy and does not flee far: it retreats only to the black water where its strength returns, and there it waits for the next fool to look down.
[concept] lore: A drowned body wedged under the waterline of an old well, kept moving by whatever the villagers dropped in it — coins, curses, and the occasional child — and it wants only to pull one more warm thing down into the dark with it.

It lies buried under the flagstones for years, listening through the ash for footfalls and schemes until a careless step comes close, then erupts in a gout of searing cinder-hot grave dust that clings and burns. Once it has prey it hunts by collapsing the ground ahead of fleeing victims, herding them back toward its smoking pit; wounded, it does not run but sinks low and burns hotter, a spiteful hoarder that would rather boil in its own fire than let a thief walk away. It is scheming (INT 14-16), and it is a relentless ambush predator.

[concept] identity: The greatsword skeleton opens by advancing in a slow, deliberate stride, dragging its blade along the ground before committing to one wide, full-body arc meant to bisect whatever is in front of it. Every round after that it plants, guards, and swings again on a flat mechanical cadence, walking down its target with the patience of something that cannot tire. It never flinches, never feints, and never retreats — it fights until its frame is shattered, indifferent to wounds that would drop a living duelist.

Verek Sarn
Medium humanoid, neutral

16 (studded leather, shield)

+3

Riposte (Reaction). When a creature within 5 feet of Verek misses it with a melee attack roll, Verek makes one handaxe attack against that creature.

Actions
Multiattack: Verek makes three Handaxe attacks. It can replace one attack with a use of Shortsword.
Handaxe: Melee or Ranged Weapon Attack: +7 to hit, reach 5 ft. or range 20/60 ft., one target. Hit: 11 (2d6 + 4) slashing damage, and Verek has advantage on its next attack roll against the target before the end of its next turn.
Shortsword: Melee Weapon Attack: +7 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. Hit: 8 (1d8 + 4) piercing damage. Immediately after the attack, a handaxe returns to Verek's grasp, and Verek has advantage on its next attack roll against the target before the end of its next turn.
Shield Bash: One creature within 5 feet that Verek can see must make a DC 15 Strength saving throw, taking 9 (2d4 + 4) bludgeoning damage on a failed save. If the target is a Medium or smaller creature, it is also knocked prone.
```

[concept] identity: Brother Halric opens a fight by bracing behind his shield and barking orders, his iron voice steadying every ally within earshot before he closes to punish the nearest foe with a measured hammer blow. Each round he holds the line — parrying strikes aimed past him, shoving enemies off his allies, and refusing to yield ground his troops depend on. Under pressure he does not flee; he anchors himself in front of the wounded and lets his authority turn a rout into a stand.
[concept] lore: Halric was a lay-brother of a frontier abbey who took up the abbey's ironbound hammer when raiders broke its doors, and he has marched with every band of survivors who would follow him since, wanting only an order worth dying into.
